# 1\. Purpose

Pumpkinzzz is a project schedule tracking application for users in the manufacturing industry to ensure machines ordered by customers are delivered to them on time. In particular:

- Each phase / milestone of the manufacturing lifecycle, from contract signing to delivery, is completed by the stipulated deadline. The user will be notified if the milestone deadline is approaching or overdue.
- The components (raw materials) are ordered by their respective stipulated deadline, so that they can arrive in time for production, taking into account delivery time. Production is considered a milestone in the manufacturing lifecycle.

For each type of product the user want to track with Pumpkinzzz, the user will have to upload the milestones and the components associated with the product type, as well as the logic / rules for calculating the various deadlines. Subsequently, if the user wants to track a product of the said type, Pumpkinzzz will automatically calculate the deadlines for the user to visualize. Moreover, Pumpkinzzz will display the milestone timeline in the dashboard in the form of Gantt Chart.

## 1.1 Project Justification

Client is currently using Excel to do said work; however, it faces difficulty in scalability and ease of use. Thus, Pumpkinzzz should be scalable to allow addition of new features easily, and user-friendly towards users with no computer backgrounds.

# 2\. Terminologies

The terminologies are simply for cross-reference with the client's original excel. The layman version of the terminologies will be used throughout the document, unless some clarifications have to be made. However, in the actual Pumpkinzzz software, the technical terminologies should be used to align with the client's business.

                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |

# 3\. Technical Details

Pumpkinzzz will be a web-based application (i.e. run on web browsers e.g. Chrome) that runs entirely locally, meaning that

- Pumpkinzzz is not hosted on any webserver. It has no domain name or public IP address and cannot be accessed via the internet.
- Pumpkinzzz is in a self-contained folder that can be zipped and sent to end users via e.g. email, after which the user may unzip the folder and run the web application
- Pumpkinzzz uses a local database that is also stored with the folder. All information uploaded by the user will be strictly kept within their local machine.
  - However, to ensure that users can exchange data between machines easily, there should be an user-friendly import and export interface. Users can export any data of interest into a simple, user-friendly format (e.g. CSV or Excel), which can be easily shared over the internet via e.g. email
- Pumpkinzzz process all the data internally with the algorithms files in the same folder. There is no API calls, and no internet connections required to use any feature of Pumpkinzzz.

Moreover, to accommodate the varying computer literacy of the clients, Pumpkinzzz should be extremely user friendly. Once the user has received and unzipped the folder, they should be able to run the application with a simple double click of a script / program. The user should not be required, under any circumstances, to perform more complicated tasks including but not limited to, using the command prompt, following a manual / README (README should be at most one line to indicate the only action that needs to be done to run the application), or downloading / setting up any requirements.

# 4\. Interfaces

This section covers the different end-user directed interfaces (i.e. GUI interactive web pages).

## 4.1 Product Type Management

This interface allows the user to manage (view, add, delete, edit, export, import) the logical information for each product type.

There are 2 types of logical information, schedule information and components information.

### 4.1.1 Schedule Information

A schedule is a partially ordered collection of milestones. A milestone is a stage or phase in the production lifecycle, for example, contract signed or production starts.

Although rare, Pumpkinzzz supports one product type having more than one schedule, depending on the circumstances (for example, normal, holiday, rush). A schedule belongs to a product type; one schedule cannot be shared across multiple product types.

All schedules must contain two default milestones:

- Contract Signed: Project starts
- ROS: Project ends

Beyond which, a schedule can have any number of custom milestones, as defined by the user.

Each milestone is associated with two dates:

- Targeted Deadline
- Actual Completion Date

(Except for Contract Signed, which has only Actual Completion Date).

When the user uploads a new product (i.e. project), the user only needs to provide its product type, schedule chosen, Date of Contract Signed, and Targeted Deadline for ROS. Then, depending on the product type and schedule chosen, Pumpkinzzz will automatically populates the project with the necessary milestones and corresponding targeted deadline. As the project continues, the user can successively upload the actual completion date of each milestone.

The targeted deadline of custom milestones can be calculated because each custom milestone is anchored to another milestone (can be either custom or default) with a number of days offset value. The two default milestones serve as the "root" and is not anchored to any milestones. For example, if milestone Production End is anchored to Production Start with an offset of 30 days, then the targeted deadline of Production End will be automatically set to be 30 days after that of Production Start. Note than offset can be both positive (occur after) or negative (occur before).

Since each custom milestone can only be anchored to exactly one other milestone and no other restrictions apply, the collection of milestones in a schedule is not strictly / linearly ordered, but rather partially ordered. This can be visualized by two tree-like hierarchy, with Contract Signed and ROS being the root of each tree.

Two database tables will be used: schedule table and milestone table.

For schedule Table, the columns are:

- Schedule ID: unique key
- Product Type ID: the product type associated with this schedule. Foreign Key
- Schedule Name: human readable schedule name for the user. Not used as unique key, but for best user experience it should be enforced that for one product type, it should not have two or more schedules sharing the same name (different product type with schedule sharing same name is fine)

For milestone table, the columns are:

- Milestone ID: unique key
- Schedule ID: foreign key
- Milestone Name: human readable name of the milestone. Not used as unique key, but for best user experience it should be enforced that for one schedule ID, milestone name should not clash
- Anchor / Basis: Milestone ID of the other milestone in the same schedule used as anchor. Needs to do data validation to ensure that its only referencing anchors with the same schedule ID
  - Can leave blank for Default milestones only
- Offset (Days): The offset of this milestone with respect to its anchor
  - A positive offset means that this milestone happens after the anchor
  - A negative offset means that this milestone happens before the anchor
- Remark: Any notes to add

As mentioned, in this interface, user can view, add, delete, edit, import, and export schedule in a user friendly manner.

- View
  - User can view all schedules together with components (see 4.1.2)
  - User can sort or filter by any reasonable column, supporting majority of the appropriate sorting / filtering conditions. For example, user can filter by a product type and immediate view the associated components as well as all the schedules for the corresponding product type.
  - User can see key information on the main pages. Once user click on a schedule, user can see the full information. Choosing what key information and how much key information to display depends on the ease of viewing.
  - The milestones will be displayed in a tree like structure, ordered on time axis to show their temporal relationships
- Add a schedule
  - User will be able to add a schedule in a user-friendly form like interface
  - Appropriate data validation needs to be checked to ensure no invalid data
  - To build the milestone tree, user should be able to do it in an intuitive, drag and drop structure so that user may immediately visualize the relationships.
  - A schedule is a collection of milestones. User do not create standalone milestones
- Delete a schedule
  - User will be able to delete a schedule in a user-friendly format
  - User can select the schedules they wish to delete, and delete all the selected schedules one-shot.
- Edit a schedule
  - For existing schedules, user can edit the schedule by adding or deleting any milestones, or edit any field in the schedule
  - User can rearrange the relationship tree for the schedule
  - Necessary data validation must be enforced
  - Similarly, user can delete milestones in a schedule in bulk.
- Export / import schedules
  - To facilitate communications between discrete users, users can choose to export or import one or more schedules (collection of milestones)
  - It will be in a csv / Excel format, which is universally understandable

### 4.1.2 Components Information

A product type has exactly one component list.

Components Table

- Component ID: unique key
- Component Name: not unique key but for user's sake should not clash
- Remarks

Product Type Components Table: With this, we can know the components list of a product type

- Component ID: foreign key
- Product Type ID: foreign key

(Neither is primary key, since one component can be used by multiple product types and one product type uses multiple components to manufacture)

Similarly, Pumpkinzzz support view, add, edit, delete, import, export of component information.

### 4.1.3 Component-Schedule Information

Pumpkinzzz uses component list to track lead time – how long it takes for a component to arrive at the factory after ordering, taking into accounts production duration, delivery duration, and any other admin duration.

Components have to arrive before the machine start production (which is a custom milestone in a schedule and can take on different names depending on the nature of production). Since each schedule has different milestones, each schedule must have its own variation of components information

- Schedule ID: foreign key
- Components ID: foreign key
- Anchor: the milestone ID that this component is related to
  - The milestone here usually means "production", as in the component must arrive before this milestone
  - Milestone ID must belong to schedule ID
- Lead time (int): the number of days required for the component to arrive after ordering
  - Thus, milestone's targeted deadline – lead time = the latest date this component has to be ordered.

When user is managing (view, edit, add, delete, export, import) the schedules, they should be able to do the same for the component-schedule information (since each schedule has its own unique copy of components information).

## 4.2 Product Registry

A product type is considered valid only if it has exactly one component list, at least one schedule, and every schedule is associated with its variation of component list.

It is considered sub-valid if at least one schedule is associated with its variation of component list, then these schedules can still be used.

Otherwise, the product type is invalid, cannot be used, and will be flagged out for user to rectify.

After the user has ensured the product type is valid or sub-valid, they can now add projects (contract for a product) under this product type.

Each new product shall have the following fields to be filled:

- **Tag No. (Compulsory, Text Box).** The unique identifier of the new product / project. Primary Key
- **Description (Optional, Text Box).** A human-readable string name of the product
  - Not used for internal calculations
- **Product Type (Compulsory, Dropdown List)**: Categorial Enumeration. Only valid or sub-valid product types will be selectable. Non valid product types can also be displayed but greyed out and not selectable.
- **Schedule ID**: Categorial Enumeration. Only schedules with associated component list will be selectable.
- **Customer (Compulsory, Text Box)**: Client's name.
  - Not used for internal calculations
- **Contract Signed (Compulsory, Date Input):** The date the contract was signed
  - The date for "Contract signed" milestone in schedule for all other milestones to base on
- **ROS (Compulsory, Date Input):** Required On Site. The hard production deadline / deployment date. This is the exact date the customer expects the machine to be delivered
  - The date for "ROS" milestone in schedule for all other milestones to base on
- **Contract No. (Compulsory, Text Box)**. The business-oriented contract number associated with this product. For Unlike Tag No., it can have repetition (not unique)
  - Not used for internal calculations
- **Sales Ref (Compulsory, Text Box).** The sales entry associated with this product. Unlike Tag No., it can have repetition (not unique)
  - Not used for internal calculations
- **Project Manager (Compulsory, Text Box):** Personnel / Organisation name
  - Not used for internal calculations
- **Engineer Owner (Compulsory, Text Box):** Personnel / Organisation name
  - Not used for internal calculations
- **Procurement Owner (Compulsory, Text Box):** Personnel / Organisation name
  - Not used for internal calculations
- **Production Owner (Compulsory, Text Box):** Personnel / Organisation name
  - Not used for internal calculations
- **FAT Owner (Compulsory, Text Box):** Personnel / Organisation name
  - Not used for internal calculations
- **Notes (Optional, Text Box):** Any additional notes
  - Not used for internal calculations

Abovementioned is the main but not the only feature of Product Registry Interface. The exhaustive list of features are as follows:

1. Uploading a new project through a manual form embedded in the page (with fields as mentioned above)
2. Download a blank CSV / Excel Template with the abovementioned fields as headers for each column, to allow user to fill multiple entries at once.
3. Continuing from point 2, once the user has filled the template, they can upload the template so that multiple entries can be uploaded at once.
   - If the format is not recognised (i.e. does not contain all required headers), the form is rejected on the spot.
   - If all headers are present, user is directed to 4.2.1 Bulk Product Registry Confirmation Interface, which displays a table / sheet like format where user can:
     - Edit any cell's value. If a field has wrong value, it will be flagged out in the table, to alert the user to correct before confirmation. This includes: compulsory field having missing value, clash in Tag No. (original value will not be cleared), not recognised date format (original value cleared since date input), not recognised product type (original value cleared since dropdown list).
     - Select any row. User can also choose to select / deselect all rows. Then, the user can choose to delete selected rows or deleted unselected rows.
     - Once the user has made the necessary corrections, they can click the upload or cancel button. Upload will only be successful if all the errors flagged out are cleared.

### 4.2.1 Bulk Product Registry Confirmation

A confirmation page redirected from, and will only redirect to, 4.2 Product Registry Interface only. Refer to section 4.2 for details.

## 4.3 Project Tracker

The project tracker interface shows all available projects in Pumpkinzzz, as well as their full list of information. User can add (redirect to product registry interface), edit, bulk import / export, delete projects.

User can see the current countdown to each milestone, as well as status (on track, urgent, extremely urgent, overdue, completed, etc). This applies for each components as well.

Moreover, this is where the user can update the actual completion date of each milestone to keep track of their current progress.

# 4.4 Dashboard

This is where the management actually sees only (in fact, project tracker interface may to too detailed or information heavy for managements to view or for presentations, its only for internal use). Dashboard extracts the most important information, depending on nature of project or agenda of meeting, and presents in a readable style.

Users can build its own dashboard by choosing what information to use, and how is it presented (numbers, tables, graphs, etc), size of each block, and arrangements of blocks, similar to Power BI. Then, user can save the dashboard (either the template or the full information) so that both can be reused, whether during meeting (full information) or other projects (template).

The heart of dashboard is Gantt Chart, vital to all projects. Gannt Chart must be available as a presentation method when building the dashboard.

# 5\. Backend / Database
